> Here is a summary of the development process for the **Standalone
  Transcription Dialog** feature, based on the previous conversation
  history.

  ### **Comprehensive Development Summary: Standalone Transcription
  Dialog**

  This document outlines the complete development lifecycle of the new
  Standalone Transcription Dialog feature. The project followed an
  iterative process, beginning with a detailed architectural plan and
  evolving through implementation, debugging, and a series of user-driven
  enhancements to deliver a robust and user-friendly speech-to-text tool.

  #### **1. Initial Planning and Architecture**

  The project began with the creation of a comprehensive architectural plan
   to ensure a modular and scalable implementation, mirroring the
  successful design of the existing Text-to-Speech (TTS) feature.

  *   **Feature Scaffolding**: A new feature module was established at
  `src/features/speech-to-text/`, complete with dedicated files for API
  logic (`api.ts`), state management (`model.ts`), TypeScript definitions
  (`types.ts`), and UI components.
  *   **State Management Design**: An Effector-based state model was
  designed to manage all aspects of the feature, including file selection,
  model configuration, loading states, error handling, and transcription
  history.
  *   **API Integration Strategy**: A plan was laid out to integrate with
  the VoidAI Speech-to-Text API, specifically targeting the
  `/v1/audio/transcriptions` endpoint using `multipart/form-data` requests
  for file uploads.

  #### **2. Core Feature Implementation**

  Following the initial plan, the core functionality of the transcription
  dialog was implemented.

  *   **UI Construction**: The primary [`TranscriptionDialog.tsx`](src/feat
  ures/speech-to-text/components/TranscriptionDialog.tsx) React component
  was built, providing the user interface for file selection, model
  configuration, and viewing results.
  *   **State and API Logic**: The Effector stores, events, and effects
  were implemented in [`model.ts`](src/features/speech-to-text/model.ts) to
   manage application state. The
  [`api.ts`](src/features/speech-to-text/api.ts) module was created to
  handle communication with the VoidAI backend.
  *   **Application Integration**: The dialog was integrated into the main
  application by adding a "Transcribe Audio" option to the chat input's
  attachment menu, making it easily accessible to users.

  #### **3. Iterative Debugging and Refinement**

  The initial implementation underwent several rounds of testing and
  debugging based on user feedback, which identified critical issues that
  were promptly addressed.

  *   **API Endpoint Correction**: An early issue where API calls were
  failing due to a duplicated `/v1/` in the request URL was identified and
  corrected by refining the endpoint construction logic.
  *   **Enhanced Error Handling**: The system was improved to provide more
  descriptive error messages. The logic was updated to parse the `detail`
  field from VoidAI's error responses and to handle network-level errors
  (e.g., "Failed to fetch") gracefully.
  *   **Major UI Regression Fix**: A significant bug where the
  transcription text and action buttons disappeared from result cards was
  traced to a CSS `overflow: hidden;` property on the parent `Card`
  component. This was fixed by applying an inline style override
  (`overflow: 'visible'`), restoring the full visibility of the card
  content.

  #### **4. User-Requested Feature Enhancements**

  A series of targeted user requests transformed the basic dialog into a
  comprehensive and powerful tool.

  1.  **Removal of Translation Functionality**: All UI elements, state, and
   API logic related to audio translation were removed after it was
  clarified that the feature should focus exclusively on transcription.
  2.  **Detailed Audio File Information**: The UI was enhanced to display
  detailed information about the uploaded audio file. Using the Web Audio
  API, the system now analyzes the file client-side and presents its
  **size, format, duration, and sample rate**.
  3.  **Native Audio Player for Preview**: To allow users to verify their
  uploads, a native HTML `<audio>` player was integrated into the dialog.
  This allows for previewing the audio file before initiating
  transcription, with proper memory management to revoke Blob URLs after
  use.
  4.  **UI Compaction**: In response to feedback, the file information
  display was redesigned from a bulky grid into a compact, single-line list
   format (`Size • Duration • Format • Sample Rate`) placed neatly
  underneath the audio player for a cleaner look.
  5.  **Response Format Selection**:
      *   A dropdown selector was added, allowing users to choose the
  desired output format (`json`, `text`, `srt`, `vtt`, `verbose_json`).
      *   The available formats are dynamically filtered based on the
  capabilities of the selected model (`whisper-1` vs. `gpt-4o` models).
      *   The user's format preference is saved on a **per-model basis** to
   `localStorage`, ensuring a personalized experience.
      *   The default format for all models was set to **text**.
  6.  **Raw Response Handling and Download**:
      *   The UI was updated to display the **raw API response** in the
  text area, allowing users to see the exact JSON, SRT, or VTT content.
      *   A **Download button** was added, enabling users to save the
  transcription in its native format with the correct file extension
  (`.txt`, `.json`, `.srt`, `.vtt`).
  7.  **Accurate Text Size Display**: The metadata display on result cards
  was corrected. Instead of showing the original audio file size, it now
  calculates and displays the size of the **resulting text transcription**,
   providing more relevant information. The duration of the original audio
  is also shown.
  8.  **Streamlined UI**: A redundant notification message that appeared
  for the `gpt-4o-mini-transcribe` model was removed to reduce interface
  clutter, as other UI elements already conveyed the necessary information.
  9.  **Improved User Workflow**: The action button on result cards was
  changed from "Send to chat" to "Paste to chat." Instead of sending the
  text directly, it now pastes the transcription into the main chat input
  and closes the dialog, allowing the user to review and edit before
  sending.
  10. **State Management Refactoring**: The component's state logic was
  completely overhauled to use the Effector store as the single source of
  truth for its visibility. This eliminated a state synchronization
  anti-pattern, fixed a critical bug that prevented the dialog from
  re-opening, and made the component more robust and maintainable.

  Sections 1-3 have been updated in Audio_Features_Integration_Plan.md.

  (END OF CONVERSATION SUMMARY)
  ---

  MY INITIAL NOTES:

  We have just implemented Phase 1, 2 and 3 of our
  Audio_Features_Integration_Plan plan. I have some feedback and new
  requirements for the next step.

  Since we have integrated Text-to-speech and Speech-to-text in separate
  dialogs, we now need to integrate it smoothly into the main chat
  messages. We would better do it in Phase 4:

  ```
  Users can send and receive audio messages:

  - **Recording**: Click microphone button to record voice message
  (Implemented)
  - **Playback**: Audio messages show inline player with controls
  (Implemented)
  - **Transcription**: Option to show/hide transcript for audio messages
  (TBD)
  - **Text-to-Speech**: Click speaker icon to hear any text message read
  aloud (TBD!!!)
  - **Multi-modal**: Mix text and audio in same conversation

  Audio message UI includes:

  - Waveform visualization (Not planned)
  - Play/pause button (Native)
  - Progress bar with time (Native)
  - Speed control (1x, 1.5x, 2x) (Native)
  - Download button (Native)
  - Transcript toggle (TBD)
  - (Note) Speaker icon to hear the text (TBD)

  Note: Text-to-speech Speaker button should generate a playable file below
   the message (as a part of it). We need a button to show/hide this
  additional file as well.

  Important note: a generated transcript and TTS audio must bot be sent to
  the chat completions endpoint with the conversation history
  (user/assistant). These temporary files should be ephemeral and be kept
  only in UI state (effector). They should be marked as "Temporary" and
  probably highlighted or colored differently somehow.

  Note: I also think we would need two new model selectors in the Chat
  settings: one for TTS model and one for STT model.
  ```

  So, do you see where it is going? We need to refactor Phase/Section 4
  before moving any further.

  Please think it through and create a comprehensive and exhaustive plan on
   how to fully integrate audio transcription in the dialog. Then update
  Audio_Features_Integration_Plan.md.

  (END OF MY INITIAL NOTES)

  ---

  **Subject: Architectural Plan for Phase 4: Integrated Audio Chat**

  Hello Claude,

  With the successful completion of our foundational audio work, including
  the standalone **TTS Dialog (Phase 2)** and **Transcription Dialog (Phase
   3)**, it is time to define the next major step: **Phase 4: Integrated
  Audio Chat**.

  The goal of this phase is to seamlessly weave our existing speech-to-text
   and text-to-speech capabilities directly into the main chat interface,
  enhancing the user experience for both text and audio messages.

  Please create a comprehensive and exhaustive architectural plan to
  implement the following features.

  ---

  ### **1. In-Message Text-to-Speech (TTS)**

  This feature allows users to generate an audio version of any text
  message.

  *   **Trigger**: An interactive **speaker icon** button shall be present
  on every text-based chat message (both user and assistant).
  *   **Action**: Upon clicking the speaker icon, the application will:
      1.  Use the pre-selected "In-Chat TTS Model" (see Configuration
  below) to generate audio for the message's text content.
      2.  Display a loading/pending state on the icon while the audio is
  being generated.
  *   **Result**:
      1.  A **temporary, inline audio player** will appear directly
  attached to (or below) the corresponding text message.
      2.  This player should have its own show/hide toggle mechanism,
  perhaps by making the speaker icon a toggle.
      3.  The generated audio is ephemeral and for immediate playback only.

  ### **2. In-Message Transcription (STT)**

  This feature allows users to get a text version of any audio message.

  *   **Trigger**: An interactive **"Transcribe" icon/button** shall be
  present on every audio message.
  *   **Action**: Upon clicking the button, the application will:
      1.  Use the pre-selected "In-Chat Transcription Model" (see
  Configuration below) to generate a transcript for the audio file.
      2.  Display a loading/pending state while the transcription is in
  progress.
  *   **Result**:
      1.  A **temporary, formatted text block** containing the
  transcription will appear directly below the audio player.
      2.  This text block should have a show/hide toggle mechanism,
  controlled by the "Transcribe" icon.

  ### **3. Core Architectural Constraints & Requirements**

  These principles are critical and apply to both TTS and STT integrations.

  *   **Ephemeral Data**: This is a crucial constraint. All generated TTS
  audio and STT transcripts are **temporary, UI-only artifacts**.
      *   They **must not** be saved as part of the persistent chat history
   in IndexedDB.
      *   They **must not** be included in the `messages` array that is
  sent to the main chat completions endpoint.
  *   **State Management (Effector)**:
      *   A new, separate Effector store must be created to manage this
  ephemeral data (e.g., `$ephemeralMessageData`).
      *   This store should be keyed by message ID to associate the
  temporary data (audio URLs, transcripts) with the correct message.
  *   **UI Indication**: The temporary nature of the generated content
  should be visually distinct. This could be achieved with a different
  background color, a dashed border, or a small "Temporary" badge.
  *   **Configuration**:
      *   Two new model selectors must be added to the **Chat Settings
  sidebar**:
          1.  **In-Chat TTS Model**: For generating speech from text
  messages.
          2.  **In-Chat Transcription Model**: For transcribing audio
  messages.
      *   The application should persist the user's selection for these
  models in `localStorage`.

  ### **4. Task: Update the Integration Plan**

  Based on these requirements, please update the [`Audio_Features_Integrati
  on_Plan.md`](Audio_Features_Integration_Plan.md). The update should
  completely revise **Section 4: Audio Input/Output in Chat** to reflect
  this new, detailed plan. The update must include:

  1.  A revised **User Experience** section.
  2.  An updated **Architecture** section with a new Mermaid diagram
  illustrating the data flow for these in-message interactions.
  3.  A detailed **State Model** section for the new ephemeral data store.
  4.  An updated **Implementation Status** section for Phase 4.

     547   ```typescript
     548 - // Features/audio-chat/model.ts structure
     549 - interface AudioChatState {
     550 -   isRecording: boolean
     551 -   recordingDuration: number
     552 -   audioBlob: Blob | null
     553 -   playbackStates: Record<string, PlaybackState>
     554 -   activePlayer: string | null
     555 - }
     548 + // Toggle TTS Audio for Text Messages
     549 + sample({
     550 +   clock: toggleMessageAudio,
     551 +   source: [$ephemeralMessageData, $inChatTtsModel],
     552 +   fn: ([ephemeralData, ttsModel], { messageId, messageText }) =>
     {
         +  {
     553 +     const currentData = ephemeralData[messageId]?.audio;
     554 +
     555 +     if (currentData?.isVisible) {
     556 +        // Hide existing audio
     557 +        return { messageId, action: 'hide' as const };
     558 +      } else if (currentData?.url) {
     559 +        // Show existing audio
     560 +        return { messageId, action: 'show' as const };
     561 +      } else {
     562 +        // Generate new audio
     563 +        return {
     564 +          messageId,
     565 +          action: 'generate' as const,
     566 +          text: messageText,
     567 +          model: ttsModel
     568 +        };
     569 +      }
     570 +    },
     571 +    target: [audioToggleProcessor],
     572 +  });
     573
     574 - interface PlaybackState {
     575 -   isPlaying: boolean
     576 -   currentTime: number
     577 -   playbackRate: number
     578 - }
     574 + // Process audio toggle actions
     575 + sample({
     576 +   clock: audioToggleProcessor,
     577 +   filter: (action) => action.action === 'generate',
     578 +   fn: ({ messageId, text, model }) => ({ messageId, text, model
     }),
         + }),
     579 +    target: generateInMessageTTSFx,
     580 +  });
     581
     582 - // Events
     583 - - recordingStarted: Begin audio recording
     584 - - recordingStopped: End recording
     585 - - audioMessageSent: Send audio to chat
     586 - - playbackToggled: Play/pause audio
     587 - - playbackRateChanged: Change speed
     588 - - transcriptToggled: Show/hide transcript
     582 + // Toggle STT Transcript for Audio Messages
     583 + sample({
     584 +   clock: toggleMessageTranscript,
     585 +   source: [$ephemeralMessageData, $inChatTranscriptionModel],
     586 +   fn: ([ephemeralData, sttModel], { messageId, audioUrl }) => {
     587 +     const currentData = ephemeralData[messageId]?.transcript;
     588 +
     589 +      if (currentData?.isVisible) {
     590 +        return { messageId, action: 'hide' as const };
     591 +      } else if (currentData?.text) {
     592 +        return { messageId, action: 'show' as const };

     741 +  // src/features/audio-chat/model.ts
     742 +  generateInMessageTTSFx.use(async ({ messageId, text, model })
     =>
         +  {
     743 +    // Reuse TTS API from src/features/text-to-speech/api.ts
     744 +    const audioBlob = await generateTTSAPI({
     745 +      text,
     746 +      model,
     747 +      voice: getDefaultVoiceForModel(model),
     748 +      format: 'mp3'
     749 +    });
     750 +

     752 +    return { messageId, audioUrl };
     753 +  });
     754 +
     755 +  generateInMessageSTTFx.use(async ({ messageId, audioUrl, model
     }
         + ) => {
     756 +    // Convert URL to File for STT API
     757 +    const response = await fetch(audioUrl);
     758 +    const audioBlob = await response.blob();
     759 +    const audioFile = new File([audioBlob], 'audio.mp3', { type:
     '
         + audio/mp3' });
     760 +
     761 +    // Reuse STT API from src/features/speech-to-text/api.ts
     762 +    const result = await transcribeAudioAPI({
     763 +      file: audioFile,
     764 +      model,
     765 +      responseFormat: 'text'
     766 +    });
     767 +
     768 +    return { messageId, transcript: result.text, format: 'text'
     };
     769 +  });
     770 +  ```
     771 +
     772 +  #### 4.4.3 Memory Management
     773 +
     774 +  **Critical**: Proper cleanup of temporary audio URLs to prevent
         + memory leaks:
     775 +
     776 +  ```typescript
     777 +  // Cleanup when component unmounts or chat changes
     778 +  const cleanupEphemeralData = createEvent<void>();
     779 +
     780 +  sample({
     781 +    clock: cleanupEphemeralData,
     782 +    source: $ephemeralMessageData,
     783 +    fn: (ephemeralData) => {
     784 +      // Revoke all blob URLs
     785 +      Object.values(ephemeralData).forEach(messageData => {
     786 +        if (messageData.audio?.url) {
     787 +          URL.revokeObjectURL(messageData.audio.url);
     788 +        }
     789 +      });
     790 +      return {};
     791 +    },
     792 +    target: $ephemeralMessageData,
     793 +  });
     794 +
     795 +  // Auto-cleanup after 1 hour
     796 +  const autoCleanupFx = createEffect(() => {
     797 +    setTimeout(() => {
     798 +      cleanupEphemeralData();
     799 +    }, 60 * 60 * 1000); // 1 hour
     800 +  });
     801 +  ```

⏺ Perfect! I've successfully created a comprehensive architectural plan for
   Phase 4: Integrated Audio Chat and updated the Audio Features
  Integration Plan. Here's a summary of what has been documented:

  Key Features Planned for Phase 4:

  1. In-Message Text-to-Speech (TTS)

  - Speaker icons (🔊) on every text message that generate temporary audio
  players
  - Toggle behavior with loading states and visual feedback
  - Ephemeral audio players that appear below messages with native HTML5
  controls
  - Audio cached temporarily for the session but never persisted

  2. In-Message Transcription (STT)

  - Transcribe buttons (📝) on every audio message that generate temporary
  transcripts
  - Toggle behavior between "Transcribe" and "Hide Transcript" states
  - Ephemeral transcript blocks with distinctive visual styling
  - Transcripts cached temporarily but never saved to chat history

  3. Chat Settings Integration

  - Two new model selectors in Chat Settings:
    - In-Chat TTS Model (default: tts-1-hd)
    - In-Chat Transcription Model (default: whisper-1)
  - Settings persisted to localStorage separately from chat data

  Critical Architectural Constraints:

  ✅ Ephemeral Data Only: Generated content must NOT be saved to
  IndexedDB✅ API Isolation: Temporary content must NOT be sent to chat
  completions endpoint✅ State Separation: Dedicated $ephemeralMessageData
  store separate from $messages✅ Memory Safety: Proper blob URL cleanup to
   prevent memory leaks

  Implementation Strategy:

  The plan leverages the existing TTS and STT API adapters from the
  completed Phases 2 and 3, ensuring code reuse and consistency. The
  architecture maintains strict separation between persistent chat data and
   ephemeral audio enhancements.

  Visual Design:

  Temporary content will be visually distinct with:
  - Dashed borders and muted background colors
  - "Temporary" badges or labels
  - Different color schemes to indicate ephemeral nature

  The comprehensive plan includes detailed Mermaid diagrams, TypeScript
  interfaces, Effector state models, and specific implementation examples.
  This should provide a clear roadmap for implementing the integrated audio
   features while maintaining the architectural integrity of the chat
  application.

     488 +   * Cleans up ephemeral data older than 1 hour
     489 +   */
     490 +  const autoCleanupFx = createEffect(() => {
     491 +    const ephemeralData = $ephemeralMessageData.getState();
     492 +    const oneHourAgo = Date.now() - (60 * 60 * 1000);
     493 +
     494 +    Object.entries(ephemeralData).forEach(([messageId, data]) =>
     {
     495 +      const audioOld = data.audio && data.audio.timestamp <
     oneHou
         + rAgo;
     496 +      const transcriptOld = data.transcript &&
     data.transcript.tim
         + estamp < oneHourAgo;
     497 +
     498 +      if (audioOld || transcriptOld) {
     499 +        clearEphemeralData(messageId);
     500 +      }
     501 +    });
     502 +  });
     503 +
     504 +  // Run auto-cleanup every 30 minutes
     505 +  setInterval(() => {
     506 +    autoCleanupFx();
     507 +  }, 30 * 60 * 1000);
     508 +
     509 +  //
     =============================================================
         + ========
     510 +  // DEBUGGING
     511 +  //

     153   /**
     154 +   * TTS Effect Implementation
     155 +   * Reuses the existing TTS API from text-to-speech feature
     156 +   */
     157 +  generateInMessageTTSFx.use(async ({ messageId, text, model })
     =>
         +  {
     158 +    try {
     159 +      // Import TTS API from existing feature
     160 +      const { generateTTS } = await
     import('../text-to-speech/api'
         + );
     161 +      const { getDefaultVoiceForModel } = await
     import('../voice-m
         + odels');
     162 +
     163 +      // Get default voice for the selected model
     164 +      const voice = getDefaultVoiceForModel(model) || 'nova';
     165 +
     166 +      // Generate audio using existing TTS API
     167 +      const audioBlob = await generateTTS({
     168 +        text,
     169 +        model,
     170 +        voice,
     171 +        format: 'mp3' as const,
     172 +        speed: 1.0,
     173 +      });
     174 +
     175 +      // Create blob URL for temporary use
     176 +      const audioUrl = URL.createObjectURL(audioBlob);
     177 +
     178 +      return {
     179 +        messageId,
     180 +        audioUrl,
     181 +        voice,
     182 +      };
     183 +    } catch (error) {
     184 +      console.error('TTS generation failed:', error);
     185 +      throw new Error(error instanceof Error ? error.message :
     'TT
         + S generation failed');
     186 +    }
     187 +  });

     190 +   * STT Effect Implementation

     193 +  generateInMessageSTTFx.use(async ({ messageId, audioUrl, model
     }
         + ) => {
     194 +    try {
     195 +      // Import STT API from existing feature
     196 +      const { transcribeAudio } = await
     import('../speech-to-text/
         + api');
     197 +
     198 +      // Convert blob URL to File object for STT API
     199 +      const response = await fetch(audioUrl);
     200 +      const audioBlob = await response.blob();
     201 +      const audioFile = new File([audioBlob], 'audio.mp3', {
     type:
         +  'audio/mp3' });
     202 +
     203 +      // Transcribe audio using existing STT API
     204 +      const result = await transcribeAudio({
     205 +        file: audioFile,
     206 +        model,
     207 +        responseFormat: 'text' as const,
     208 +        prompt: '', // No context prompt for in-message
     transcript
         + ion
     209 +      });
     210 +
     211 +      return {
     212 +        messageId,
     213 +        transcript: result.text || result.toString(),
     214 +        format: 'text',
     215 +      };
     216 +    } catch (error) {
     217 +      console.error('Transcription failed:', error);
     218 +      throw new Error(error instanceof Error ? error.message :
     'Tr
         + anscription failed');
     219 +    }
     220 +  });

⏺ I found the issue! The problem is with the quote marks in the CSS content
   property. In CSS-in-JS, we need to properly escape quotes. The content
  property should use single quotes around the double quote character. Let
  me fix this:


⏺ Update(src/features/audio-chat/components/EphemeralTranscript.tsx)

  ⎿  Updated src/features/audio-chat/components/EphemeralTranscript.tsx with
      2 additions and 2 removals

  ⎿  Waiting…

     > cp node_modules/pdfjs-dist/build/pdf.worker.min.mjs

        ▲ Next.js 15.2.4

⏺ Perfect! The build completed successfully.

  ✅ Fixed CSS Quote Escaping Issue

  The problem was with the CSS content property in the EphemeralTranscript
  component. The issue was:

  🔧 Root Cause:

  In the &::before and &::after pseudo-elements, I was using:
  content: '"',  // ❌ This caused CSS parsing issues

  🛠️ Solution:

  I replaced it with properly escaped Unicode quote characters:
  '&::before': {
    content: '"\u201C"',  // ✅ Left double quotation mark
    color: 'text.secondary',
  },
  '&::after': {
    content: '"\u201D"',  // ✅ Right double quotation mark
    color: 'text.secondary',
  },

  📋 What This Fixes:

  - Eliminates the malformed CSS that was causing browser parsing errors
  - Prevents the weird content display you saw in the inspector
  - Maintains the intended quotation styling around transcript text
  - Ensures proper CSS-in-JS quote escaping

  The ephemeral transcript component now renders properly with clean CSS
  and the intended quotation marks around the transcript text, while
  maintaining all the improved visual styling we implemented.

  audioChatDomain.createStore<EphemeralMessageData>({});

        generateInMessageTTSFx.use(async ({ messageId, text, model }) => {
          const { generateSpeech } = await import('../text-to-speech/api');
          const ttsResult = await generateSpeech({ text, model, voice,
  format: 'mp3', speed: 1.0 });
          const audioBlob = new Blob([ttsResult.audio], { type: 'audio/mp3'
   });
          const audioUrl = URL.createObjectURL(audioBlob);
          return { messageId, audioUrl, voice };
        });
        ```

  actions

  characteristics. I'll focus on creating a modular solution that allows
  for type-specific rendering while keeping the core structure consistent.

  FileAttachmentWrapper that supports multiple file types, with specialized
   renderers for each file category. The delete functionality will be a
  critical feature, ensuring clean removal of attachments from both the

⏺ ✅ Problem Fixed!

  I've successfully resolved the issue with expand buttons opening
  about:blank for attached images. Here's what I implemented:

  The Solution

  Created a shared openImageInNewTab helper function that properly handles
  data URLs by:

  1. Detecting data URLs: Checks if the image URL starts with data:
  2. Converting to blob URLs: Uses fetch() to convert data URLs to blob
  objects, then creates a blob URL with URL.createObjectURL()
  3. Opening in new tab: Opens the blob URL, which browsers handle much
  better than data URLs
  4. Memory cleanup: Automatically revokes the blob URL after 1 second to
  prevent memory leaks
  5. Fallback handling: If anything fails, falls back to trying the
  original URL

  Applied Everywhere

  The fix now works for:
  - ✅ Regular attached images (both expand button and clicking on image)
  - ✅ Generated images (both expand button and clicking on image)
  - ✅ All image types that use data URLs

  How It Works

  // Before: window.open(dataUrl, "_blank") → about:blank
  // After: Converts data URL → blob URL → opens properly

  const openImageInNewTab = async (imageUrl: string) => {
    if (imageUrl.startsWith('data:')) {
      const response = await fetch(imageUrl);
      const blob = await response.blob();
      const blobUrl = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
      window.open(blobUrl, '_blank'); // This works!
      // Cleanup after 1 second
      setTimeout(() => URL.revokeObjectURL(blobUrl), 1000);
    }
  };

  Now when users click the expand button (🔍) or click on any attached
  image, it will properly open the full-size image in a new tab instead of
  showing about:blank!

  ✅ Problem Fixed: Single Pending Message for Multiple Files

  I've successfully resolved the issue where file attachments were creating
   separate pending messages. Here's what I changed:

  Root Cause

  The processFilesFx effect was creating a separate message for each file
  by pushing individual messages to an array and returning Message[].

  Solution Implemented

  1. Modified processFilesFx signature:
    - Before: effect<File[], Message[]> (returns multiple messages)
    - After: effect<File[], Message> (returns single message)
  2. Unified processing logic:
    - All files (images, audio, documents) are now processed into single
  arrays:
        - contentParts: MessageContentPart[] - contains all file content
  parts
      - attachments: Attachment[] - contains all file attachments
    - Creates one message with all files combined
  3. Updated store handlers:
    - Before: [...messages, ...newImageMessages] (spread array of messages)
    - After: [...messages, newFileMessage] (add single message)
  4. Fixed auto-select model logic:
    - Updated to work with single message instead of array

  Result

  Now when users select multiple files (e.g., 3 images + 1 PDF + 2 audio
  files), they get:

  ✅ One pending message with all 6 attachments
  ❌ Six separate pending messages

  The unified FileAttachmentWrapper ensures all file types look consistent
  within the single message, and the delete functionality works properly
  for removing individual attachments from the combined message.


> **Subject: Refactor: Consolidate All File Attachments into a Single
  Pending Message**

  We've identified an important workflow issue concerning file attachments
  within the main chat interface that needs immediate attention before
  proceeding with further multimodal integrations.

  **1. The Problem: Multiple Pending Messages for Attachments**

  Currently, when a user attaches files in multiple operations (e.g., first
   an image, then an audio file, or several batches of files), each
  attachment operation results in a *separate* "pending" message appearing
  in the chat window. This creates visual clutter and a fragmented user
  experience.

  *   **Observation**: While uploading multiple files *at once* correctly
  groups them into a single pending message, subsequent upload operations
  (`filesSelected` events) create entirely *new* pending messages.
  *   **Root Cause Analysis (My Understanding)**: The `processFilesFx`
  effect (in
  [`src/features/chat/model.ts`](src/features/chat/model.ts:199)) always
  generates a new unique message ID (`crypto.randomUUID()`) for each batch
  of files processed. The subsequent handling then simply appends this
  newly generated message to the main `$messages` store. There is no
  mechanism in place to identify an *existing, active* pending attachment
  message and append new files to it. The current bundling logic only
  occurs when the message is explicitly sent, but not during the "pending"
  state management.

  **2. Desired Behavior: A Single, Unified Pending Message**

  My clear requirement is that regardless of how many separate file
  attachment operations a user performs, all attached files (images, audio,
   documents, recordings) **must coalesce into a single, unified pending
  message.** This single message should persist until the user explicitly
  sends it (either by typing text and clicking send, or sending the
  media-only message).

  *   There should be **only one (and only one) pending user message** at
  any given time for attachments.
  *   New attachments should *always* update this single active pending
  message, not create new ones.

  **3. Proposed Refactoring Plan: Single Source of Truth for Pending
  Multimodal Messages**

  To address this, we need to refactor the logic responsible for handling
  file attachments and their representation in the chat `$messages` store.
  The goal is to introduce a single source of truth for the active pending
  multimodal message.

  **3.1. Introduce a Dedicated Store:**
  *   Create a new Effector store: `$activePendingMultimodalMessage =
  chatDomain.store<Message | null>(null)`. This store will hold the
  *single* `Message` object that represents all current pending attachments
   and optionally the user's draft text.

  **3.2. Refactor `processFilesFx.doneData` Handling:**
  *   Modify the handler for `processFilesFx.doneData` (the event that
  emits the processed file message) to *not* directly append a new message
  to `$messages`.
  *   Instead, this handler should:
      1.  Check the current state of `$activePendingMultimodalMessage`.
      2.  If `$activePendingMultimodalMessage` is `null` (or invalid for
  merging), the `Message` returned by `processFilesFx` becomes the new
  `$activePendingMultimodalMessage`.
      3.  If a valid `$activePendingMultimodalMessage` already exists, the
  content (files/attachments) from the `Message` returned by
  `processFilesFx` should be *merged into* the existing
  `$activePendingMultimodalMessage` object. The `id` of the existing
  pending message must be preserved.
      4.  Update the `timestamp` of the `$activePendingMultimodalMessage`
  to reflect the latest modification.

  **3.3. Update `$messages` Store from `$activePendingMultimodalMessage`:**
  *   The main `$messages` store should then be updated to accurately
  reflect the state of `$activePendingMultimodalMessage`. This means:
      1.  If `$activePendingMultimodalMessage` becomes non-null, ensure
  it's present in `$messages`. If an older version exists, replace it.
      2.  If `$activePendingMultimodalMessage` becomes `null`, ensure it's
  removed from `$messages`.
      3.  Crucially, ensure no other "pending" attachment messages remain
  in `$messages` if they were somehow created incorrectly (though the new
  flow should prevent this).

  **3.4. Refine `messageSent` Logic:**
  *   The `messageSent` sample (lines 763-886 in
  [`src/features/chat/model.ts`](src/features/chat/model.ts:763)) which
  currently tries to `find consecutive pending media messages at the end`
  and bundle them, will need to be simplified.
  *   It should directly take `$activePendingMultimodalMessage` as its
  source (if it's not null) and combine its content with the `$messageText`
   to form the final `userMessageCreated` payload.
  *   After `userMessageCreated` fires (signaling the message has been
  sent), `$activePendingMultimodalMessage` must be reset to `null`.

  **3.5. Consider Edge Cases:**
  *   Ensure that if the user *only* attaches files (without typing text),
  and then explicitly clicks a "Send" button (if one exists for media-only
  messages), this still correctly finalizes and sends the
  `$activePendingMultimodalMessage`.
  *   Address any potential race conditions during rapid file attachments.

  **4. Deliverables:**

  Please provide a detailed architectural plan covering the necessary
  Effector events, stores, and effects, and how they will interact to
  achieve this single-pending-message behavior. Include any necessary
  Mermaid diagrams to illustrate the revised data flow.

  ---

              const response = await fetch(imageUrl);
              const blob = await response.blob();
              const blobUrl = URL.createObjectURL(blob);
              window.open(blobUrl, '_blank');

            } else {
              window.open(imageUrl, '_blank');
